# Facts

The only list of established facts in this project. Rules and statuses: `01-evidence-rules.md`. Newest first within each section. Nothing is `MEASURED` or `CONFIRMED` without hardware evidence in `evidence/`.

## Hardware behaviour (our unit)

Unit A: ASRock BC-250, BIOS P3.00 (12/09/2021), PCI `1002:13FE`. All entries below: evidence `evidence/linux/2026-09-21-E01-diagusb-run-001/` (diagnostic USB, probes id `79152824`, kernel 6.18.52-0-lts), 2026-09-21.

| # | Claim | Status | Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | The regcalc offsets address the registers they are named after (S1, S2 hold on hardware) | CONFIRMED | Independent controls, all read through the raw sysfs `resource5` mmap: (a) after amdgpu's ring test `SCRATCH_REG0` at `0x30100` holds `0xDEADBEEF`, the value the kernel's GFX ring test writes to the register it calls `SCRATCH_REG0`; (b) `MMMC_VM_FB_LOCATION_BASE/TOP = 0xF400/0xF5FF` and `HDP_NONSURFACE_BASE = 0xF4000000` agree with dmesg `VRAM: 8192M 0x000000F400000000 - 0x000000F5FFFFFFFF`; (c) `CC_GC_SHADER_ARRAY_CONFIG = 0xFFF80000` and, after init, `SPI_PG_ENABLE_STATIC_WGP_MASK = 0x7` are the stock values published independently by `bc250-40cu-unlock`; (d) driver-owned registers change the way the driver's init implies: `CP_ME_CNTL 0x15000000 -> 0`, `CP_MEC_CNTL 0x50000000 -> 0`, `RLC_CNTL 0 -> 1`, page table base and IH ring base `0 ->` non-zero, `CP_RB0_RPTR == CP_RB0_WPTR = 0x600`, `IH_RB_RPTR == IH_RB_WPTR = 0xAA0`. Raw reads and debugfs `amdgpu_regs` reads agree for all 58 registers, which shows the two access paths are equivalent but is not by itself a naming control (both take the same byte offset) |
| M2 | The GPU's MMIO is readable and writable from the host before any GPU driver has loaded | MEASURED | No driver bound (`driver: ''`, PCI command `0006`). `GRBM_STATUS = 0x00003028`; `SCRATCH_REG0` read back both written patterns (`0xCAFEDEAD`, `0x5A5AA5A5`) and was restored; 28 of 58 registers non-zero. Same method as M1, so the pre-driver values are trustworthy. Linux only: says nothing yet about Windows (E02) |
| M3 | `GRBM_GFX_INDEX` bank select works from raw MMIO before the driver | MEASURED | SE0/SA0 and SE0/SA1 selected and restored to broadcast; both banks read the same values (M4) |
| M4 | This unit runs the stock compute-unit configuration | MEASURED | `CC_GC_SHADER_ARRAY_CONFIG = 0xFFF80000` in both shader arrays, before and after amdgpu. `SPI_PG_ENABLE_STATIC_WGP_MASK = 0x0000FFFF` as the BIOS leaves it, `0x00000007` after amdgpu init. `RLC_PG_ALWAYS_ON_WGP_MASK = 0x3`, `GC_USER_SHADER_ARRAY_CONFIG = 0`, `CC_RB_BACKEND_DISABLE = 0` throughout. No sign of a 40 CU unlock in the state the BIOS hands over |
| M5 | `GB_ADDR_CONFIG` reads `0x00000044`, before and after amdgpu init, raw and through debugfs | MEASURED | Differs from `CYAN_SKILLFISH_GB_ADDR_CONFIG_GOLDEN = 0x00100044` in bit 20. Why is open; do not use the golden value as a positive control for this register |
| M6 | State handed over by the BIOS: CP halted, RLC stopped, no page table, SMU answering | MEASURED | Before the driver: `CP_ME_CNTL = 0x15000000`, `CP_MEC_CNTL = 0x50000000`, `RLC_CNTL = 0`, `MMVM_CONTEXT0_PAGE_TABLE_BASE = 0`, `IH_RB_BASE = 0`, `CP_RB0_BASE = 0xFEDCBAEF`; `MP1_SMN_C2PMSG_90 = 1`; `MP0_SMN_C2PMSG_81 = 0x002AFC44` and `C2PMSG_64 = 0x80000000`. VRAM carve-out already programmed: `MMMC_VM_FB_LOCATION_BASE/TOP = 0xF400/0xF5FF`, `HDP_NONSURFACE_BASE = 0xF4000000` |
| M7 | Mainline amdgpu initializes this unit without patches | MEASURED | `modprobe amdgpu` returned 0 after 8.5 s; 12 rings; VRAM 8192M at `0xF400000000`, GTT 3834M; firmware ME 0x63, PFP 0x94, CE 0x25, RLC 0x0D, MEC 0x90, SDMA 0x34. After init `CP_ME_CNTL = 0`, `CP_MEC_CNTL = 0`, `RLC_CNTL = 1`. The only `*ERROR*` lines are display HPD / dummy IRQ noise |
| M8 | The IP discovery table of the unit matches `cyan_skillfish_ip_offset.h` for the blocks we use | MEASURED | GC 10.1.3 bases `0x1260, 0xA000, 0x02402C00`; MMHUB 2.0.3 `0x1A000`; OSSSYS 5.0.1 `0x10A0`; HDP 5.0.1 `0xF20`; MP0/MP1 11.0.8 `0x16000`; NBIF 2.1.1. Full table in `report.json` |
| M9 | What the predecessor's offsets really hold on hardware | MEASURED | `0x3260` ("GRBM_STATUS") = 0 while the real `GRBM_STATUS` at `0x8010` = `0x3028`; `0x9C1C`, `0x5C3C`, `0xDA60`, `0xE060` = 0; `0x3D64` = `0xFFFFFFFF`; `0x34D0` = `0xBA062100`; `0x4A74` = `0xFFFBD9FB`. None of them behaves like the register it was taken for |
| M10 | The ASUS USB-AC58 works on the BC-250 with the stock Alpine kernel: WPA2-PSK association, DHCP, SSH | MEASURED | `wpa_state=COMPLETED`, CCMP, 2437 MHz, first configured network; SSH session from the dev PC used to collect this evidence. Observed live, not part of the evidence directory |
| M11 | Idle in a bare console the GPU sits at 81-82 C and 60-61 W | MEASURED | hwmon `temp1_input`, `power1_average`, three samples about 4 minutes after boot; no fan sensor exposed. Observed live over SSH. Cooling must be sorted out before long sessions |
| M12 | Under mainline amdgpu all 12 rings execute commands, including the KIQ | MEASURED | `evidence/linux/2026-09-21-E01-recon/mqd/*.ptrs.txt`: rptr == wptr == driver wptr on every ring (gfx `0x600`, kiq `0xE00`, 8 compute `0x200`, sdma0 `0x4C0`, sdma1 `0xF0`). MQDs of the gfx and compute queues captured as hex words in the same directory: a reference for our own queue setup. Contradicts the predecessor's "KIQ is hardwired to 0" (R3) |
| M13 | The firmware exposes no IOMMU to the OS | MEASURED | No IVRS ACPI table, 0 IOMMU groups (`iommu.txt`, `acpi-tables.txt`). A Windows driver on this unit deals with physical DMA addresses only |
| M14 | Platform facts for the Windows side | MEASURED | UEFI 2.6 (AMI), Secure Boot off, BIOS P3.00; 16 x 1 GiB memory chips, split 8 GiB VRAM / 7.85 GiB system; GPU BARs: 0 = 256 MiB (resizable to 1 GiB), 2 = 2 MiB doorbells, 4 = I/O, 5 = 512 KiB MMIO; MSI-X with 3 vectors; PCIe reports 16 GT/s x16; VBIOS `113-AMDRBN-003` is delivered through the ACPI `VFCT` table (no PCI ROM BAR); ACPI tables and VBIOS captured outside the repo (`REDACTED.txt` in the recon directory says where) |
| M15 | Power: the SMU offers three SCLK levels (1000/1500/2000 MHz), the unit idles at the middle one, 906 mV, about 67 W for the whole SoC; nearly all GFX clock gating is off | MEASURED | `gpu-sysfs.txt`, `debugfs-amdgpu_pm_info.txt`. Overdrive range 1000-2000 MHz, 700-1129 mV. Explains M11 |
| M16 | A read of `MMHUB.MMEA0_ADDRDEC0_BASE_ADDR_CS0` (BAR5 `0x68594`) hangs the machine | MEASURED | `evidence/linux/2026-09-21-E03-init-trace/sweep-before-run1-GC-complete-then-hang.log` (last, unfinished line); two earlier hangs the same day without a surviving log are consistent with it. Rule: never read BAR5 addresses that are not named registers on an allow-list |
| M17 | Reference state and init sequence of a working driver | MEASURED | Same directory: 5797 named registers before any driver, 5021 after amdgpu init, 1096 differ (936 GC, 116 MMHUB); ordered trace of amdgpu's init with 10018 register writes and 136117 reads; ring buffers and MQDs. The trace holds no host write to `SPI_PG_ENABLE_STATIC_WGP_MASK` or `CC_GC_SHADER_ARRAY_CONFIG`, although the former changes `0xFFFF -> 0x7` during init |
| M18 | Under Windows, with only the Basic Display driver on the GPU, BAR5 MMIO reads work from a kernel driver and return the same values as under Linux before amdgpu | MEASURED | `evidence/windows/2026-09-21-E02-run-001/`: 5542 named registers read, none hung, 5074 bit-identical to the Linux pre-driver sweep, among them every key register of M4-M6 (`GRBM_STATUS 0x3028`, `CC_GC_SHADER_ARRAY_CONFIG 0xFFF80000`, `SPI_PG_ENABLE_STATIC_WGP_MASK 0xFFFF`, `CP_ME_CNTL 0x15000000`, `CP_RB0_BASE 0xFEDCBAEF`). `GRBM_READ_ERROR` is 0 after the sweep |
| M19 | The 468 registers that differ are not a Windows effect | MEASURED for the pattern, HYPOTHESIS for the cause | 457 of them differ in 1-5 bits of random-looking values (context registers `PA_*`, `CB_*`, `SPI_*`, `COMPUTE_*`, `GRBM_SCRATCH_REG1-7`, `CP_DMA_WATCH*`): consistent with flops that nothing initializes and that power up slightly differently each time. The rest are clocks/timestamps and three PSP mailbox registers whose values match the earlier Linux session *after* amdgpu, i.e. PSP state that survived a restart. To confirm: a second sweep after a recorded cold start |
| M20 | Windows assigns the GPU's BARs differently from Linux | MEASURED | BAR0 `0xC0000000`, BAR2 `0xD0000000`, BAR5 `0xFE800000` (Linux: `0xF400000000`-range BAR0 is the VRAM aperture, BAR5 `0xFE900000`); command register `0x0406`. A driver must take addresses from its resource list, never from constants |
| M21 | Unit A boots Windows 11 from NVMe; the M.2 link is intermittent with this SSD | MEASURED | Booted through the fallback loader of a disk the firmware had never seen. Before that, with the SSD installed, root port `00:15.0` showed `LnkSta 0x1100` (no link partner) and the firmware did not list the disk; after one more restart it worked, nothing else changed (journal 2026-09-21) |
| M22 | The SMU mailbox works under Windows without any AMD driver, and clock/voltage requests take effect | MEASURED | `evidence/windows/2026-09-21-E04-smu-underclock/`: `TestMessage(41)` returns 42; SMU version `0x00580600`; firmware default is 1500 MHz at vid 101 (about 919 mV, Linux showed 906 mV at the same level); `RequestGfxclk 1000` then `ForceGfxVid 116` (820 mV) are acknowledged and read back through `GetGfxFrequency` / `GetGfxVid`. Mailbox protocol and register offsets taken from our own amdgpu init trace (E03), message numbers and limits from `cyan_skillfish_ppt.c`. This is also the first write to the GPU function under Windows: it sticks |
| M23 | SoC temperature under Windows is readable through the SMN index/data pair of the root complex | MEASURED | `THM_TCON_CUR_TMP` (SMN `0x59800`) via `00:00.0` config `0x60/0x64`, the path of Linux' k10temp. 73 C idle, 82 C during first-boot servicing at 1500 MHz, falling after the underclock. Plausibility only: no independent sensor at the same moment |
| M24 | amdgpu's init on unit A, ordered: no host write to any microcode port (firmware goes in through 11 PSP ring commands in 35 ms), about 900 non-display writes in the order GART, IH, PSP, SMU tables, golden registers, RLC, un-halt CP/MEC, KIQ by MMIO, SDMA, interrupts; two thirds of all writes are display-core AUX traffic. Corrected the same day: the 99 844 polls of `MMVM_INVALIDATE_ENG17_SEM` at the first MMHUB TLB flush (0.21 s, dmesg timeout) are **an artifact of our own pre-driver sweep**, not amdgpu's normal init on this unit, see M25 | MEASURED (order and values), meaning of the steps from source | `docs/init-sequence.md`, `docs/init-sequence-unitA.generated.md` (from the E03 trace via `tools/trace/summarize_init.py`) |
| M25 | Reading is not side-effect free: a read of any `GCVM_INVALIDATE_ENGn_SEM` / `MMVM_INVALIDATE_ENGn_SEM` **acquires** that invalidation semaphore (first read 1, every later read 0 until somebody writes 0), `CP_*_HEADER_DUMP` registers advance on each read, and a read of `GRBM_GFX_CNTL` latches `GRBM_READ_ERROR` (`0x80008088`: error bit plus that register's address) | MEASURED | `evidence/windows/2026-09-21-E05-run-001/comparison.txt`: two sweeps with nothing changed in between, 36 registers differ. Cross-check under Linux: the E03 pre-driver sweep read `MMVM_INVALIDATE_ENG17_SEM = 1` and amdgpu then timed out on exactly that semaphore, while E01 (no full sweep before the driver) has no such timeout in its dmesg. Consequences: these registers leave the sweep allow-lists; `GRBM_READ_ERROR = 0` in M18 was read before the offending register in sweep order and says nothing about the sweep as a whole; M19's Windows-vs-Linux differences have to be re-read with this in mind |
| M26 | A display-only WDDM driver can own `1002:13FE` on unit A and live on the firmware's framebuffer: install without reboot, 1920x1200 kept, no GC register changed by the switch, survives disable/enable and a restart, uninstall returns the device to Basic Display | MEASURED | Experiment E05 with Microsoft's unmodified KMDOD sample as the instrument, `evidence/windows/2026-09-21-E05-run-001/` |
| M27 | Lab facts for the Windows side: KDNET works over the on-board RTL8168 (`busparams 4.0.0`, the OS then uses the debugger's virtual adapter); safe mode with networking has network and SSH over that wired path but not over the USB Wi-Fi dongle (vendor driver not loaded there); with a debugger attached the ACPI interpreter reports AML errors `0x3a` once and `0x78` several times at every boot (firmware tables, not investigated); one warm restart ended in a black screen before the kernel started and needed the reset button, four others went through | MEASURED | journal 2026-09-21; debugger log kept outside the repo |
| M28 | Our own miniport `bc250kmd` (display-only, no MMIO) owns `1002:13FE` on unit A: install without reboot, 1920x1200 on the firmware's framebuffer, breadcrumbs reach stage 61 in order, no GC register outside the noise set changes, survives disable/enable and a restart (start budget 1 -> 2 -> confirmed to 0 by `bc250mon`), rollback to Basic Display and re-install both work. Milestone M3 reached | MEASURED | Experiment E06, `evidence/windows/2026-09-21-E06-run-001/` |
| M29 | `D3DKMTEscape` with `D3DKMT_ESCAPE_DRIVERPRIVATE` **does reach `DxgkDdiEscape` of a display-only driver**: an elevated user-mode tool gets `STATUS_SUCCESS` and the driver's data back (`BC250_ESCAPE_GET_INFO`). Without an escape handler of ours the same call returns `STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER` (Basic Display) or `STATUS_NOT_SUPPORTED` (KMDOD sample). `D3DKMTOpenAdapterFromDeviceName` accepts only interface paths of `GUID_DISPLAY_DEVICE_ARRIVAL` | MEASURED | `evidence/windows/2026-09-21-E06-run-001/kmd-after-install-080531.txt`, `evidence/windows/2026-09-21-escape-probe/` |
| M30 | Register writes work under Windows through our miniport, at regcalc's addresses: `SCRATCH_REG0` (`0x30100`) and `SCRATCH_REG1` (`0x30104`) took `0xCAFEDEAD` / `0x0BC25001` written through a gated escape of `bc250kmd`, an independent driver (`bc250rd`, own mapping) read the same values back, and a full GC sweep shows no other register changed. Reads through both drivers agree bit for bit. With the gates closed, or for offsets outside the generated tables, the driver refuses | MEASURED | Experiment E07, `evidence/windows/2026-09-21-E07-run-001/` |
| M31 | Under Windows the VRAM carve-out is reachable from the CPU by system physical address, exactly as amdgpu reaches it: `GCMC_VM_FB_OFFSET << 24` = `0x270000000`, length 8 GB from `GCMC_VM_FB_LOCATION_BASE/TOP`, MC base `0xF400000000`. The range is outside every range of `MmGetPhysicalMemoryRanges` (Linux's e820 map of the same unit marks it reserved). BAR0 (`0xC0000000`, where the firmware framebuffer is) shows the same memory from VRAM offset 0: framebuffer pixels and a test page read identically through both paths, words written through one path read back through the other. The top 2 MB, where amdgpu keeps its GART table on this unit, read without fault. All 147 VM/MC location, aperture, context 0 and L2 registers of GC and MMHUB read the same under Windows as in the firmware state amdgpu started from | MEASURED | Experiment E08, `evidence/windows/2026-09-21-E08-run-001/`; register comparison from the sweeps of E02, E07 and E03 |
| M32 | BAR0 is not a coherent view of VRAM: after a word was written through BAR0 and then changed through the physical address, BAR0 kept returning its own last value for at least 45 s (both mappings uncached on the CPU side), and agreed again a few reads later. The BAR0 path goes through HDP, which amdgpu flushes explicitly. The driver therefore accesses VRAM by physical address only | MEASURED (staleness); INFERRED (that HDP is the cause) | E08 `write-x-091041.txt`, `hdp-stale-091140.txt` |
| M33 | GART and VM context 0 come up under Windows with AMD's own code: `gfxhub_v2_0.c` and `mmhub_v2_0.c`, imported unmodified from mainline v6.18 and run inside `bc250kmd` against our shim, issue the same 285 register writes in the same order as amdgpu did on this unit (values equal except four registers that carry the addresses of our scratch and dummy pages); both engine-17 TLB invalidations are acknowledged, the MMHUB semaphore protocol works, no protection fault status bit is set, the registers read back through an independent driver as written (two self-clearing, as under Linux), the hardware's own responses (`SDMA0/1_UTCL1_INV0`, request/acknowledge) equal Linux's state after init, and the firmware's scan-out keeps running. Page table: top 2 MB of VRAM, MC `0xF5FFE00000`, where amdgpu puts it. The firmware state can be written back (282 registers); six status registers keep the record of the invalidation. On the kernel that produced the E03 trace (6.18.52) `gmc.noretry` is true for GC 10.1.3, on mainline v6.18 it would be false; the driver uses the measured value | MEASURED | Experiment E09, `evidence/windows/2026-09-21-E09-run-001/`; host replay test `driver/shim/test` |
| M34 | The PSP takes the GPU firmware under Windows, through AMD's own `psp_v11_0_8.c` (imported unmodified) and amdgpu's command sequence run inside `bc250kmd`: ring create, `SETUP_TMR` (4 MB at MC `0xF5FF800000`) and ten `LOAD_IP_FW` (SDMA0, SDMA1, CE, PFP, ME, MEC1, MEC1 jump table, MEC2, MEC2 jump table, RLC), every fence returned with status 0, the 15 register writes equal to amdgpu's on this unit in offset, order and value, command times equal to amdgpu's spacing (0.13 ms jump tables, 1.9 ms SDMA, 5.9 ms CP). Images staged in VRAM, not behind the GART. The response carries a TMR address for the CP images only (CE `+0x42000`, PFP `+0x83000`, ME `+0xC4000`, MEC1 and MEC2 `+0`); for SDMA, jump tables and RLC it is 0. `DESTROY_TMR` and ring stop are accepted (`C2PMSG_64 = 0x80030000` afterwards) and a second ring, TMR and load are accepted in the same boot | MEASURED | Experiment E10, `evidence/windows/2026-09-21-E10-run-001/`; host replay test `driver/shim/test/replay_psp.c` |
| M35 | Loading through the PSP is not passive: the PSP starts what it loads. After the ten `LOAD_IP_FW` and with no other register written by us, `SDMA0/1_F32_CNTL` read 0 instead of 1 (halt released, `SDMA0/1_STATUS2_REG` changes from sweep to sweep), `RLC_CNTL` reads 1 and 31 GC registers hold the value Linux shows after amdgpu's init (RLC power-gating and SERDES state, `CGTS_*_SM_CTRL_REG`, `CC_GC_SHADER_ARRAY_CONFIG_GEN0`, `RLC_SMU_COMMAND = 0x13`: the RLC has talked to the SMU). `CP_ME_CNTL` (`0x15000000`) and `CP_MEC_CNTL` (`0x50000000`) do not change: the CP stays halted until the driver releases it. An unload (`DESTROY_TMR`, ring stop) does not undo this; a warm reboot does. A second load in the same boot meets the running RLC and leaves it disabled and busy (`RLC_CNTL = 0`, `RLC_STAT = 5`, `GRBM_STATUS2` bit 24), the former claim that amdgpu avoids this by stopping RLC is unproven: the generic SMU cleanup has a conditional stop callback, but E13 reload also failed and its unload traces lack that write (M333) | MEASURED | Experiment E10, `comparison.txt` in `evidence/windows/2026-09-21-E10-run-001/` |
| M36 | The GPU executes commands under Windows: amdgpu's `gfx_v10_0_hw_init()` and `sdma_v5_0_hw_init()` for this part, transcribed against AMD's unmodified tables and structures (`driver/shim/bc250_gfx.c`, `bc250_sdma.c`) and run inside `bc250kmd` after GART enable (M33) and the PSP load (M34), bring up RLC, KIQ, MEC, eight compute queues and the gfx queue, and all eleven ring tests pass: a `SET_UCONFIG_REG` packet written by the CPU into each ring changes `SCRATCH_REG0` from `0xCAFEDEAD` to `0xDEADBEEF` (milestone M5) | MEASURED | E11, `evidence/windows/2026-09-21-E11-run-001/`. 355 register writes, 341 equal to amdgpu's on this unit in offset, order and value, 14 address registers carrying our addresses, none other; 14 doorbells; stage 6 (CP) takes 349 us. Witness (`bc250rd`) afterwards: `SCRATCH_REG0 = 0xDEADBEEF`, `CP_ME_CNTL = 0`, `CP_MEC_CNTL = 0`, `CP_STAT = 0`, `CP_RB0_RPTR = CP_RB0_WPTR = 0x500`, `GRBM_STATUS = 0x00003028`, no protection fault bit in either hub, 593 registers at Linux's after-init value, picture undisturbed. Needed beforehand and nothing else: `RCC_DOORBELL_APER_EN = 1` (0 under Windows), GART, PSP-loaded firmware. No SMU message from the driver. The GRBM CAM alias (`VGT_ESGS_RING_SIZE_UMD` -> `VGT_ESGS_RING_SIZE`) is set up by the firmware under Windows too. Repeated in a second boot with all stages in one call. SDMA: rings programmed and engines released, not tested (no SDMA ring test yet). |
| M37 | GPU access to system memory through the GART works under Windows with the CPU physical address as bus address: rings, write-back slots and EOP buffers are contiguous non-paged system memory entered into the GART table of M33 with AMD's PTE format (`0x0003000000000077` flags: valid, system, snooped, read, write, execute, MTYPE UC), and the CP fetches and executes from them | MEASURED | E11: 23 binds (0x121000 bytes), each followed by a VMID 0 TLB flush of both hubs as amdgpu does (69 writes, every flush acknowledged); ring tests pass, read and write pointers advance to `0x500`, no protection fault. Settles the inference of the lab check (no VBS, no hypervisor, no DMA remapping for this device). The undo works on hardware: queues unmapped through the KIQ (4 doorbells), `CP_ME_CNTL = 0x15000000`, `CP_MEC_CNTL = 0x50000000`, `SDMAn_F32_CNTL = 1`, `RLC_CNTL = 0`, memory returned; a device restart with running engines does the same by itself. After such a halt `GRBM_STATUS` reads `0xA0003028` and `GRBM_STATUS2` `0x30000008` until the machine is rebooted. A second bring-up after the undo is not possible yet: nothing unmaps the KIQ's own HQD (upstream has no such step either), INFERRED from code and the host test, not tried on hardware (see M39: done on hardware with one declared deviation). |
| M38 | Windows gives a display-only WDDM miniport a message-signalled interrupt for this function: with the documented `Interrupt Management\MessageSignaledInterruptProperties` values (`MSISupported = 1`, `MessageNumberLimit = 1`) in the INF's `.HW` section the translated interrupt resource is a message (`CM_RESOURCE_INTERRUPT_MESSAGE`) from the first device start after the install, no reboot needed (0.5.x without the section: line interrupt, IRQ 52). With no interrupt source enabled the interrupt routine is never called: not over register sweeps, presents, device restarts, nor while RLC, CP and SDMA run and execute ring tests with the IH ring off | MEASURED | E12 part A, `evidence/windows/2026-09-21-E12-run-001/`: vectors 0x80, 0x51, 0x70 across starts, 0 calls throughout. Whether `DxgkCbQueueDpc` works for a display-only miniport is not answered yet (no interrupt, no DPC) |
| M39 | A second GFX bring-up in the same boot works after the undo of M37, with one declared deviation from amdgpu: where `gfx_v10_0_kiq_init_register()` finds the KIQ's HQD still active and the MEC halted, the driver writes `CP_HQD_ACTIVE = 0` instead of a dequeue request that no running engine could answer (`driver/amdgpu-import/PROVENANCE.md`; upstream's only such write is SR-IOV's, `gfx_v10_0.c:7029`) | MEASURED | E12 run 001: run, undo, run again, undo; all seven stages rc 0 both times, all ring tests pass, no register outside the generated table. 358 writes against 355: `RLC_SPM_MC_CNTL` skipped (already holds the value), four more in the HQD branch (`CP_HQD_ACTIVE`, `CP_HQD_PQ_RPTR = 0x737`, `CP_HQD_PQ_WPTR_LO/HI`), and six first writes carry bits the first run left in the register (`CP_HQD_PQ_CONTROL` bit 15, `CP_HQD_PERSISTENT_STATE` bits 18 and 19, `SDMAn_CNTL` bit 5, `SDMAn_GFX_RB_CNTL` bit 12: all read-modify-write). Stage 6 (CP) takes 5222 us in the second run against 349 us in the first: explained by M44 (a stale fetch walking the old ring), which also shows that this second bring-up only worked because the new KIQ ring had the old one's address |

| M137 | The shader page is mapped in VRAM, and the dispatch that does not retire raises a UTCL2 fault and an SQ error on VMID 1. Fresh boot 2026-09-22T21:51:42, bc250kmd 0.7.48.1 (0x00070030), same gates as M121, same job frame, same ICD as M136. `fence gfx 1 ib` returned in 32 us, FETCHED. One unclamped `fill_g1` timed out: `HARDWARE FENCE TIMEOUT after 500 ms (sequence 6): fence 34`, `t_submit_to_idle_us` 501088.3, hash `0xeb05052ea5b62325`. The new line is `wddm: shader 0x100040000 lo 01000400 hi 00000000 -> phys 0x271C8A000 vram leaf 0x271C8A071 dram 00000000 00000000 00000000 00000000`. Leaf low 12 bits `0x071` are VALID, EXECUTABLE, READABLE and WRITEABLE, and not SYSTEM (`AMDGPU_PTE_*`). The IB page on the same submit is leaf `0x14B120073`, which is those flags plus SYSTEM. The four zero dwords are not a VRAM read: `VidMmProbeIb` copies bytes only for a system page. `ih state` before teardown consumed 7 vectors. VMID 1 has client 0x1b (`SOC15_IH_CLIENTID_UTCL2`) source 0 (`UTCL2_1_0__SRCID__FAULT`) and client 0x0a (`SOC15_IH_CLIENTID_SE0SH`) source 239 (`GFX_10_1__SRCID__SQ_INTERRUPT_ID`), 44 ns apart. The SQ payload `00100000 00000080` has encoding bits 38:39 equal to 2 (`SQ_INTERRUPT_WORD_ENCODING_ERROR`) and `ERR_TYPE` bits 19:22 equal to 2. No header consulted here names error type 2. No VMID 1 CP end-of-pipe (source 181). The fault payload was not decoded as an address. No TDR (Display 4101 = 0), no bugcheck, undo back to stage 61 display-only, UnconfirmedStarts confirmed 0. The shader fetch page is mapped. A wave ran, and VMID 1 faulted. M8 is not reached | MEASURED (n = 1 bring-up, 1 submit) | `evidence/windows/2026-09-22-M8-vulkan-shader-pte/` | 2026-09-22 |

## Refuted claims from prior art

| # | Claim | Status | Why | Date |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R3 | "KIQ_BASE / KIQ_CNTL / CP_RING0_BASE_LO registers exist and are hardwired to 0" | REFUTED (by source) | No such registers in `gc_10_1_0_offset.h`; pinned by `test_regcalc.py` | 2026-09-21 |
| R2 | "Linux can write these registers only thanks to debugfs privilege / an early boot window" | REFUTED (by source) | debugfs uses the driver's normal accessors; the unlock patch runs at ordinary module init; a runtime tool exists | 2026-09-21 |
| R1 | "`SPI_PG_ENABLE_STATIC_WGP_MASK` lives at BAR5+0x5C3C (or 0x34FC), `CC_GC_SHADER_ARRAY_CONFIG` at 0x9C1C (or 0x3264)" | REFUTED (by source and by measurement, M1 + M9) | regcalc: `0x935C` and `0x89BC`; details in `predecessor-analysis.md` | 2026-09-21 |

Claims such as "registers are SOS-locked" or "NBIO is locked at EFI boot" are neither confirmed nor refuted as hardware behaviour: they were measured at wrong offsets and are simply unsupported. Under Linux nothing is locked before the driver loads (M2, M3); the same reads under Windows are experiment E02.

| M583 | The 44 CTS cases dEQP-VK.api.external.semaphore.opaque_win32.* and .opaque_win32_kmt.* (cts-release-tools deqp-vk AE7BEFDD, one process per case, loader debug log kept) give the same result on the registered ICD 93B1D1FD and on the fence-share candidate 6661C2D2: 4 Pass (the info_binary and info_timeline queries), 40 NotSupported ("Semaphore doesn't support exporting in external type"), 0 Fail, 0 Crash, 0 timeout; every process loaded wsi-final/vulkan_radeon.dll. Cause of the NotSupported, read from the info logs and the runtime source: the exportable and importable bits are advertised only for timeline semaphores with OPAQUE_WIN32 or D3D12_FENCE (vk_wddm2_monitored_fence_type exports NT handles), binary semaphores use the vk_sync_binary wrapper, which has no import or export, and OPAQUE_WIN32_KMT is never advertised by vk_semaphore.c. The 40 functional cases are binary-semaphore tests, so the CTS exercises the fence-share change only through the info query; its runtime witness stays M581 (vkd3d-proton D3D12_FENCE export). Same boot, same KMD 153, CPU DWM 4596, within one hour: vkcube on the CPU present path copies a 1920x1181 image in 1.07 ms (8.5 GB/s) on the host-cached candidate 50E99A84, on E43's 6661C2D2 and on the witness 63AF86CB, and a 640x480 image in 0.14 ms (E46 level); the steered Witcher 3 DX12 scene copies 1920x1200 in 18.3 ms median (run 009, 3201 presents) and 17.2 ms median (run 010, 2688 presents) with a floor of 16.9-17.9 ms and no value near vkcube's. The witness ICD shows the blit buffer in RADV memory type 5, properties 0xe (HOST_VISIBLE, COHERENT, CACHED) in both processes, and QueryThreadCycleTime around the copy gives 54.3 million cycles per game frame against 3.45 million in vkcube for the same byte count, CPU share 1.00 in both (0.17 versus 2.6 bytes per cycle). The game's page faults are 0-22/s in the scene (side sampler). 50E99A84's HOST_CACHED request was a no-op because the port sets wsi->sw and upstream already selects a cached type; with sw the WSI waits for the image fence before the present, so copy time is memcpy time. Open: the physical placement (segment) and Lock2 cache attribute of the game's 9.2 MB blit buffer, not visible from the ICD (HYPOTHESIS: uncached BAR or non-aperture placement in the game process; 0.5 GB/s is the shape of an uncached read stream). Registered ICD 93B1D1FD restored after every stage, smoke PASS, no KMD change. | MEASURED (unit A) | [E48](../evidence/windows/2026-09-27-E48-witcher3-dx12-cpu-copy-witness/RESULT.md) | 2026-09-27 |
